Currently it doesn't.\n\nWe need to fix the export functionality to include totals row when requested.\n\nPossible approach: Modify export_csv to accept an optional totals parameter (default False). If totals=True, compute totals using column_totals(rows), then append a row that marks it as total. So it seems render_rows when mark_last=True will take the last row and prepend \"TOTAL\" to it? Or replace the first column with \"TOTAL\"? Actually the last row values are 3 and 4. The output row is \"TOTAL,3,4\". So it seems the first column is replaced with the string \"TOTAL\", and the rest are the values from the last row. That's odd.\n\nBut the test_export_appends_totals_row expects:\n\"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"\nHere the totals row is \"TOTAL,4,6\". The values 4 and 6 are the column totals: first column total 1+3=4, second column total 2+4=6. So the totals row contains the totals for each column, with \"TOTAL\" in the first column.\n\nSo there are two different totals rows: one that marks the last row as total (maybe for highlighting), and one that appends a row with actual totals.\n\nThe test test_export_appends_totals_row expects the totals row to be appended with the computed totals.\n\nThus, the correct behavior when totals=True is to compute column totals and append a row where the first column is \"TOTAL\" and the rest are the totals.\n\nNow, how is this currently implemented? It's not. We need to implement it.\n\nLooking at the files, we have column_totals in totals.py that computes sums, but doesn't handle non-numeric cells. We have render_rows that just joins values. We have export_csv that just calls render_rows.\n\nWe need to modify export_csv to handle totals parameter. When totals=True, we should compute the totals using column_totals, then append a row that is [\"TOTAL\"] + totals_values, then render all rows including the original rows and the totals row.\n\nBut render_rows currently doesn't support marking rows specially. However, we could modify render_rows to accept an optional parameter to indicate which row is the total row, or we could handle it in export_csv by manually constructing the last row string.\n\nAlternatively, we could change render_rows to support a mark_last parameter that when True, transforms the last row to start with \"TOTAL\". But the test test_render_marks_last_row_as_total expects that behavior for mark_last=True. So perhaps render_rows should be fixed to support mark_last=True, and then export_csv when totals=True could compute totals, append a row with totals values, and then call render_rows with mark_last=True on the last row? But the totals row is not the last row of the original data; it's an additional row. So mark_last might not be appropriate.\n\nBetter: Modify export_csv to check if totals=True argument is passed. If so, compute totals = column_totals(rows), then create a new row [\"TOTAL\"] + totals, append to rows, then call render_rows on the combined rows. If totals is False or not provided, just call render_rows on original rows.\n\nBut export_csv currently only takes one argument rows. We need to change its signature to accept an optional totals parameter, or maybe accept *args and kwargs.\n\nLooking at the test call: export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True). This suggests export_csv accepts keyword arguments. So we should modify export_csv to accept **kwargs and handle totals=True.\n\nAlternatively, we could modify the function to accept second parameter defaults to False.\n\nLet's examine the actual code in export.py again. Maybe there is more to it that we haven't seen because the read tool only showed part of the file? But we read the whole file; it's short.\n\nMaybe the file has changed since the test was written, and now export_csv doesn't accept totals parameter, causing the test to fail?
